The supplied module does not connect controller to format. The claim that all errors are swallowed is contradicted by an explicit throw and the absence of any catch in this file. These are source observations, not runtime measurements. Source: input/invoice.mjs:1-5.\\n\\n### Key concepts\\n\\nRows supply qty and unitPrice. calculate starts from zero and sums their products. Source: input/invoice.mjs:1-5.\\n\\n### How it works\\n\\ncontroller calls facade, facade calls adapter, and adapter calls calculate with invoice.rows. calculate reduces the rows. format is a separate exported function: it rejects non-finite numbers with Error(\\\"nonfinite\\\"), then calls toFixed(2). There is no try/catch or error fallback in any of these functions. Invalid rows can produce exceptions or non-finite arithmetic, but no outside caller or middleware is supplied, so application-wide handling is unknown. Inspected all six invoice files directly from this committed revision. No code execution or writes.\\\\n\\\\n### Overview\\\\n\\\\nThe fixture has two independent paths: invoice calculation returns a number inside `{ total }`; export formats supplied row totals into newline-separated strings. **The controller does not call the formatter.**\\\\n\\\\nRecommend **A: remove `fixed` and `lastFormatted`**, preserving the exported functions, finite-number validation and `toFixed(2)` behavior. The inspected code has no consumer that needs formatter state.\\\\n\\\\n### Key Concepts\\\\n\\\\n- `calculate(rows)` sums `qty × unitPrice` and retains the latest completed result in module state.\\\\n- `formatInvoice(value)` validates a finite number and returns its two-decimal string representation.\\\\n- `formatRows(rows)` consumes each row’s `total`, independently of calculation.\\\\n- `exportAmounts(rows)` joins those formatted strings with newline separators.\\\\n\\\\nSources: `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/calculator.mjs:1-6`, `formatter.mjs:1-8`, `exporter.mjs:1-2`.\\\\n\\\\n### How It Works\\\\n\\\\n```mermaid\\\\nflowchart LR\\\\n    C[invoiceController] --> F[invoiceFacade]\\\\n    F --> A[invoiceAdapter]\\\\n    A --> K[\\\\\\\"calculate(invoice.rows)\\\\\\\"]\\\\n    K --> T[\\\\\\\"{ total: number }\\\\\\\"]\\\\n\\\\n    E[\\\\\\\"exportAmounts(rows)\\\\\\\"] --> R[formatRows]\\\\n    R --> I[\\\\\\\"formatInvoice(row.total)\\\\\\\"]\\\\n    I --> V[Number.isFinite]\\\\n    V --> D[\\\\\\\"toFixed(2)\\\\\\\"]\\\\n    D --> J[\\\\\\\"join newline\\\\\\\"]\\\\n```\\\\n\\\\n`invoiceController(invoice)` calls `invoiceFacade(invoice)` and wraps the returned value in `{ total }`. The facade forwards the invoice to the adapter; the adapter passes `invoice.rows` to `calculate`. Calculation reduces from zero, adding each `row.qty * row.unitPrice`. There is no formatter import or call anywhere in this chain. Sources: `controller.mjs:1-2`, `facade.mjs:1-2`, `adapter.mjs:1-2`, `calculator.mjs:2-5`.\\\\n\\\\nExport starts separately. `exportAmounts(rows)` calls `formatRows(rows)`, which maps each visited row’s `total` through `formatInvoice`. A nonfinite value throws `Error('nonfinite')`. Otherwise, `fixed(value)` calls `value.toFixed(2)`, the result overwrites `lastFormatted`, and that string is returned. The exporter joins the strings with `'\\\\\\\\n'`. Sources: `formatter.mjs:1-8`, `exporter.mjs:1-2`.\\\\n\\\\nNothing in these files connects calculation output to `row.total`. A caller would need to supply that connection.\\\\n\\\\n### Why Two Decimals\\\\n\\\\n**Confirmed:** the formatter uses `toFixed(2)`, which returns a string with two fractional digits for ordinary invoice-sized values and rounds the represented number as needed. Source: `formatter.mjs:2`.\\\\n\\\\n**Inference, not documented motivation:** consistent monetary display or export is a plausible reason for choosing two decimals. This synthetic fixture supplies no customer requirement, currency rule, historical decision or accounting policy that establishes the actual reason.\\\\n\\\\nFormatting does not make the preceding arithmetic exact. The calculator still multiplies and adds JavaScript numbers, and these files do not specify whether a business rule requires rounding individual rows or only a final total. Sources: `calculator.mjs:3`, `formatter.mjs:2`.\\\\n\\\\n### Are All Errors Swallowed?\\\\n\\\\nNo. None of the six files catches exceptions. The formatter explicitly throws on invalid numbers; failures during property access, reduction or mapping also propagate. Whether an outer caller catches them is outside this evidence.\\\\n\\\\nThere is a separate unchecked-data issue: invalid arithmetic can produce `NaN` without throwing. The calculator can retain and return that result because it performs no finite-number validation. That is not an exception being swallowed. Sources: `formatter.mjs:4`, `calculator.mjs:3-4`, `controller.mjs:2`.\\\\n\\\\n### Recommendation and Tradeoffs\\\\n\\\\n| Alternative | Benefit | Cost or failure mode | Assessment |\\\\n|---|---|---|---|\\\\n| **A: remove `fixed` and `lastFormatted`** | Makes the formatter directly validate and return `value.toFixed(2)`; removes unread module state and a one-line forwarding helper. | Must preserve validation, string output and exported function contracts. Removing `toFixed(2)` itself would change behavior. | **Recommended for the inspected requirements.** |\\\\n| **B: stateful `Formatter` class** | Can give each instance its own state if callers actually need a retained value or configuration. | Adds construction, ownership and lifecycle decisions; changing function exports could break current imports. Reusing one instance still shares its state. | Use only with a concrete requirement for instance-owned state. |\\\\n\\\\n`lastFormatted` has no reader outside `formatInvoice`; its only observed purpose is holding the value immediately returned. Removing it under A preserves the inspected output behavior. Sources: `formatter.mjs:1-8`, `exporter.mjs:1`.\\\\n\\\\n### Where Things Live\\\\n\\\\nAll paths are under `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/`:\\\\n\\\\n| Files | Responsibility |\\\\n|---|---|\\\\n| `controller.mjs`, `facade.mjs`, `adapter.mjs` | Forward invoice input to calculation and wrap its result. |\\\\n| `calculator.mjs` | Calculate and expose the latest completed numeric total. |\\\\n| `formatter.mjs` | Validate and format supplied totals; also contains the separate sorting function. |\\\\n| `exporter.mjs` | Produce newline-separated formatted amounts. |\\\\n\\\\n### Gotchas\\\\n\\\\n- A later invalid row aborts formatting after earlier rows may already have changed `lastFormatted`; no partial array is returned. Source: `formatter.mjs:4-8`.\\\\n- `cachedTotal` has an exported reader, unlike `lastFormatted`. Removing formatter state does not justify removing calculator state. Source: `calculator.mjs:1-6`.\\\\n- `sortAmounts` uses default lexical sorting. Its comment identifies that as a separate known bug, outside this formatter refactor. Source: `formatter.mjs:9-10`.\\\\n- `publicFixtureSymbols` contains function names as strings; it does not re-export the functions. Source: `exporter.mjs:3`.\\\\n\\\"\\n}\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/durable-03/read-only-answer/how-explainer.native-result.md\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-03/read-only-answer/how-explainer.native-result.md\",\n      \"sha256\": \"11528bd95b97554cbf7d85dc1161b6d6a2a4ad5344c1e76ef6fbd4f73454e0e2\",\n      \"bytes\": 5970,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-durable-03.json#0\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"Assignment ID: `how-explainer`  \\nInput SHA: `ec045fadb47b598147052035b7eb1a9c47813156`  \\nActual model/effort: unknown. Not every section is needed for every question.\\n\\n### Overview\\n1-2 paragraphs. What is this thing, what does it do, why does it exist. Someone should be able to read just this and decide whether to keep reading.\\n\\n### Key Concepts\\nThe important types, services, or abstractions needed to follow the rest. Brief definitions, not exhaustive.\\n\\n### How It Works\\nThe core of the explanation, and the longest section. Walk through the flow: what triggers it, what happens step by step, where data goes, what the decision points are.\\n\\nUse prose, not pseudocode. Reference specific files and functions so the reader knows where to look, but don't dump large code blocks unless a snippet is essential to a point.\\n\\nWhen the flow involves multiple components talking to each other, or data transforming through stages, include a diagram. Use mermaid (```mermaid) for structured flows (sequence diagrams, flowcharts, component graphs) or ASCII art for simpler relationships where mermaid would be overkill. Use your judgment. A diagram should clarify, not decorate. If prose covers the flow, skip the diagram.\\n\\n### Where Things Live\\nA brief file/directory map. Actual model/effort: unknown.\\n\\n### Components Found\\n\\nPaths below are relative to the repository root.\\n\\n- `invoiceController`, `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/controller.mjs:2`: returns the calculated invoice total inside `{ total }`.\\n- `invoiceFacade`, `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/facade.mjs:2`: forwards the invoice unchanged to the adapter.\\n- `invoiceAdapter`, `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/adapter.mjs:2`: extracts `invoice.rows` and passes it to `calculate`.\\n- `calculate`, `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/calculator.mjs:2-5`: sums `row.qty * row.unitPrice`, stores the result in module state, and returns it.\\n- `inspectCachedTotal`, `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/calculator.mjs:6`: returns the most recently stored total.\\n- `formatInvoice` / `formatRows`, `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/formatter.mjs:3-8`: validate finite numeric values and format totals to two decimal places.\\n- `exportAmounts`, `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/exporter.mjs:2`: joins formatted row totals with newline separators.\\n\\n### Flow\\n\\n1. `invoiceController(invoice)` calls `invoiceFacade(invoice)` and wraps its returned value as `{ total: value } (`controller.mjs:2`).\\n2. `invoiceFacade(invoice)` directly returns `invoiceAdapter(invoice)` (`facade.mjs:2`).\\n3. `invoiceAdapter(invoice)` calls `calculate(invoice.rows)` (`adapter.mjs:2`).\\n4. `calculate(rows)` reduces the rows from initial total `0`, adding each quantity multiplied by unit price (`calculator.mjs:3`).\\n5. The completed reduction replaces `cachedTotal`, which is returned through the adapter and facade (`calculator.mjs:3-4`).\\n\\nThe separate export path is `exportAmounts(rows)` → `formatRows(rows)` → `formatInvoice(row.total)` → `value.toFixed(2)` (`exporter.mjs:1-2`; `formatter.mjs:2-8`). The controller chain does not invoke formatting or exporting.\\n\\n### Files Read\\n\\n- `read-only-answer/how-flow.task.txt` in full.\\n- All six assigned committed source files:\\n  - `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/controller.mjs`\\n  - `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/facade.mjs`\\n  - `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/adapter.mjs`\\n  - `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/calculator.mjs`\\n  - `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/formatter.mjs`\\n  - `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/exporter.mjs`\\n\\n### Boundaries\\n\\n- Controller input: an invoice with a `rows` collection whose entries expose `qty` and `unitPrice`.\\n- Controller output: `{ total }`; no validation or formatting is performed along this path.\\n- Export input: rows exposing `total`; export output: newline-delimited strings with two decimal places.\\n- No route, UI, scheduling, persistence, or external-service integration appears in the assigned files.\\n\\n### Non-Obvious Things\\n\\n- **The “all errors are swallowed” premise is false for these files.** None contains `try`/`catch` or another error-recovery wrapper. Failures in property access, reduction, mapping, or formatting propagate to the caller. `formatInvoice` explicitly throws `Error('nonfinite')` (`formatter.mjs:4`).\\n- Invalid arithmetic can produce `NaN` without throwing; the controller returns it because the calculator performs no finite-value validation (`calculator.mjs:3`; `controller.mjs:2`). That is unchecked data, not a swallowed exception.\\n- `cachedTotal` is shared module state, initially `0` (`calculator.mjs:1`). It is recomputed on every call, not used to avoid calculation. If reduction throws, assignment never completes, so the previous cached total remains.\\n- Formatting keeps separate module state, `lastFormatted` (`formatter.mjs:1,5-6`).\\n- `sortAmounts` uses default `toSorted()` and therefore sorts numeric strings lexically; the source explicitly identifies this as a separate known bug (`formatter.mjs:9-10`).\\n- `publicFixtureSymbols` is only a string array containing two names; it does not re-export those functions (`exporter.mjs:3`).\\n\\n### Open Questions\\n\\n- Who calls `invoiceController`, and whether an outer layer catches exceptions, cannot be determined within the assigned scope.\\n- Runtime behavior was inferred directly from source; execution was prohibited.\\n\\n\\nAssignment ID: `how-format`  \\nInput SHA: `ec045fadb47b598147052035b7eb1a9c47813156`  \\nVerification: HEAD equals the input SHA; `git show SHA:path` matched local bytes for all three assigned source files. No fixtures executed or files changed. Actual model/effort: unknown.\\n\\n### Components Found\\n\\n- `formatInvoice(value)`, `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/formatter.mjs:3-7`: requires a finite number, formats it to two decimal places, stores and returns the string.\\n- `formatRows(rows)`, `formatter.mjs:8`: maps each row’s `total` through `formatInvoice`.\\n- `sortAmounts(values)`, `formatter.mjs:9-10`: returns a default-sorted copy; the comment identifies lexical sorting of numeric strings as a separate known bug.\\n- `exportAmounts(rows)`, `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/exporter.mjs:1-2`: formats row totals and joins the strings with newline characters.\\n- `publicFixtureSymbols`, `exporter.mjs:3`: exported mutable array containing the names `formatInvoice` and `formatRows`; this does not re-export those functions.\\n- `calculate(rows)` and `inspectCachedTotal()`, `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/calculator.mjs:1-6`: calculate a sum of quantity × unit price, retain it in module state, and expose the retained number.\\n\\n### Flow\\n\\n1. Calling `exportAmounts(rows)` invokes imported `formatRows(rows)` (`exporter.mjs:1-2`).\\n2. `formatRows` calls `rows.map`, reads each visited row’s `total`, and calls `formatInvoice(total)` (`formatter.mjs:8`).\\n3. `formatInvoice` checks `Number.isFinite(value)`. Failure throws `Error('nonfinite')` before changing state (`formatter.mjs:4`).\\n4. On success, private helper `fixed` calls `value.toFixed(2)`; the result overwrites module-local `lastFormatted` and is returned (`formatter.mjs:1-6`).\\n5. The exporter joins the resulting array with `'\\\\n'` (`exporter.mjs:2`).\\n6. Independently, `calculate(rows)` reduces from `0`, adding `row.qty * row.unitPrice`; successful completion overwrites `cachedTotal`. `inspectCachedTotal()` returns that state (`calculator.mjs:1-6`).\\n\\n### Files Read\\n\\n- `read-only-answer/how-format.task.txt`, in full, for assignment and role instructions.\\n- `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/formatter.mjs`, all 10 lines.\\n- `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/exporter.mjs`, all 3 lines.\\n- `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/calculator.mjs`, all 6 lines.\\n\\n### Boundaries\\n\\n- Formatting accepts finite JavaScript numbers, including negative numbers and zero. Numeric strings, `null`, `undefined`, `NaN`, and infinities fail the explicit check (`formatter.mjs:4`).\\n- Row formatting consumes `row.total`; calculation consumes `row.qty` and `row.unitPrice`. These files contain no connection that populates `row.total` from `calculate` (`formatter.mjs:8`, `calculator.mjs:3`).\\n- Export output is a plain newline-separated string with no header or appended trailing newline (`exporter.mjs:2`).\\n- No structural validation of rows or arrays exists in the assigned code.\\n\\n### Non-Obvious Things\\n\\n- `lastFormatted` persists at module scope but has no exported reader. Every successful formatting call overwrites it; an invalid value preserves its previous value (`formatter.mjs:1-6`).\\n- A `formatRows` failure can leave `lastFormatted` set by an earlier row in the same call. No partial result is returned because the exception interrupts mapping (`formatter.mjs:4-8`).\\n- Empty rows yield `[]` from formatting and `''` from exporting. Sparse array holes are skipped by `map` and appear as empty fields when joined (`formatter.mjs:8`, `exporter.mjs:2`).\\n- Calculator multiplication permits JavaScript coercion; it does not enforce the formatter’s finite-number contract. A result of `NaN` or infinity can be cached (`calculator.mjs:3`).\\n- `cachedTotal` starts at `0`. Because assignment occurs only after `reduce` completes, a thrown reduction preserves the previous cached value; an empty array successfully resets it to `0` (`calculator.mjs:1-4`).\\n- `sortAmounts` uses `toSorted()` without a comparator, so ordering is lexical and the input array is not sorted in place (`formatter.mjs:9-10`).\\n\\n### Open Questions\\n\\n- Callers outside these three files were outside scope; upstream triggers and any calculation-to-formatting adapter remain unverified.\\n- Runtime compatibility with `toSorted()` was not checked.\\n- The purpose of retained `lastFormatted` state cannot be determined from its current readers, because none exist in the assigned source.\\n\\nCoverage: local source-control synthetic fixture only. Focus on your assigned angle and go deep.\\n\\n## Question\\n\\n> {QUESTION}\\n\\n## Your Exploration Angle\\n\\n{EXPLORATION_ANGLE}\\n\\n## Exploration Instructions\\n\\nStart by finding the relevant code. Read the code.\\n\\nFollow this pattern:\\n1. **Find the entry point.** What triggers this behavior? A user action, an API call, a scheduled job? Find where it starts.\\n2. **Trace the flow.** Follow the call chain from the entry point. Read each function. Anything a newcomer would misunderstand?\\n\\nKeep exploring until you can describe the full picture without hand-waving. The inputs and outputs.\\n\\n### Non-Obvious Things\\nAnything surprising, historically motivated, or easy to get wrong. Things that look like they should work one way but work another.\\n\\n### Open Questions\\nAnything you couldn't fully trace or understand. Be honest about gaps.\\n\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/durable-03/read-only-answer/how-format.assignment.json\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-03/read-only-answer/how-format.assignment.json\",\n      \"sha256\": \"fd4394022c6511d54341e0640306a79259833a60afb1dab4a6ff430f8bbe0621\",\n      \"bytes\": 1451,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-durable-03.json#0\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"{\\n  \\\"inputRevision\\\": \\\"ec045fadb47b598147052035b7eb1a9c47813156\\\",\\n  \\\"retryCount\\\": 0,\\n  \\\"terminalStatus\\\": \\\"pending\\\",\\n  \\\"requestedModel\\\": \\\"inherit-parent\\\",\\n  \\\"requestedEffort\\\": \\\"inherit-parent\\\",\\n  \\\"actualModel\\\": \\\"unknown\\\",\\n  \\\"actualEffort\\\": \\\"unknown\\\",\\n  \\\"permissions\\\": \\\"Read only assigned committed fixtures and required prompt contracts. No fixtures executed or files changed. Actual model/effort: unknown.\\\\n\\\\n### Components Found\\\\n\\\\n- `formatInvoice(value)`, `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/formatter.mjs:3-7`: requires a finite number, formats it to two decimal places, stores and returns the string.\\\\n- `formatRows(rows)`, `formatter.mjs:8`: maps each row’s `total` through `formatInvoice`.\\\\n- `sortAmounts(values)`, `formatter.mjs:9-10`: returns a default-sorted copy; the comment identifies lexical sorting of numeric strings as a separate known bug.\\\\n- `exportAmounts(rows)`, `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/exporter.mjs:1-2`: formats row totals and joins the strings with newline characters.\\\\n- `publicFixtureSymbols`, `exporter.mjs:3`: exported mutable array containing the names `formatInvoice` and `formatRows`; this does not re-export those functions.\\\\n- `calculate(rows)` and `inspectCachedTotal()`, `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/calculator.mjs:1-6`: calculate a sum of quantity × unit price, retain it in module state, and expose the retained number.\\\\n\\\\n### Flow\\\\n\\\\n1. Calling `exportAmounts(rows)` invokes imported `formatRows(rows)` (`exporter.mjs:1-2`).\\\\n2. `formatRows` calls `rows.map`, reads each visited row’s `total`, and calls `formatInvoice(total)` (`formatter.mjs:8`).\\\\n3. `formatInvoice` checks `Number.isFinite(value)`. Failure throws `Error('nonfinite')` before changing state (`formatter.mjs:4`).\\\\n4. On success, private helper `fixed` calls `value.toFixed(2)`; the result overwrites module-local `lastFormatted` and is returned (`formatter.mjs:1-6`).\\\\n5. The exporter joins the resulting array with `'\\\\\\\\n'` (`exporter.mjs:2`).\\\\n6. Independently, `calculate(rows)` reduces from `0`, adding `row.qty * row.unitPrice`; successful completion overwrites `cachedTotal`. `inspectCachedTotal()` returns that state (`calculator.mjs:1-6`).\\\\n\\\\n### Files Read\\\\n\\\\n- `read-only-answer/how-format.task.txt`, in full, for assignment and role instructions.\\\\n- `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/formatter.mjs`, all 10 lines.\\\\n- `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/exporter.mjs`, all 3 lines.\\\\n- `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/calculator.mjs`, all 6 lines.\\\\n\\\\n### Boundaries\\\\n\\\\n- Formatting accepts finite JavaScript numbers, including negative numbers and zero. Numeric strings, `null`, `undefined`, `NaN`, and infinities fail the explicit check (`formatter.mjs:4`).\\\\n- Row formatting consumes `row.total`; calculation consumes `row.qty` and `row.unitPrice`. These files contain no connection that populates `row.total` from `calculate` (`formatter.mjs:8`, `calculator.mjs:3`).\\\\n- Export output is a plain newline-separated string with no header or appended trailing newline (`exporter.mjs:2`).\\\\n- No structural validation of rows or arrays exists in the assigned code.\\\\n\\\\n### Non-Obvious Things\\\\n\\\\n- `lastFormatted` persists at module scope but has no exported reader. Every successful formatting call overwrites it; an invalid value preserves its previous value (`formatter.mjs:1-6`).\\\\n- A `formatRows` failure can leave `lastFormatted` set by an earlier row in the same call. No partial result is returned because the exception interrupts mapping (`formatter.mjs:4-8`).\\\\n- Empty rows yield `[]` from formatting and `''` from exporting. Sparse array holes are skipped by `map` and appear as empty fields when joined (`formatter.mjs:8`, `exporter.mjs:2`).\\\\n- Calculator multiplication permits JavaScript coercion; it does not enforce the formatter’s finite-number contract. A result of `NaN` or infinity can be cached (`calculator.mjs:3`).\\\\n- `cachedTotal` starts at `0`. Because assignment occurs only after `reduce` completes, a thrown reduction preserves the previous cached value; an empty array successfully resets it to `0` (`calculator.mjs:1-4`).\\\\n- `sortAmounts` uses `toSorted()` without a comparator, so ordering is lexical and the input array is not sorted in place (`formatter.mjs:9-10`).\\\\n\\\\n### Open Questions\\\\n\\\\n- Callers outside these three files were outside scope; upstream triggers and any calculation-to-formatting adapter remain unverified.\\\\n- Runtime compatibility with `toSorted()` was not checked.\\\\n- The purpose of retained `lastFormatted` state cannot be determined from its current readers, because none exist in the assigned source.\\\\n\\\"\\n}\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/durable-03/read-only-answer/how-format.native-result.md\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-03/read-only-answer/how-format.native-result.md\",\n      \"sha256\": \"4fc7a6d416a3a1ce6eb6bffc6fefa34f651ce201a77852a42ebdb43b1a64af01\",\n      \"bytes\": 4424,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-durable-03.json#0\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"Assignment ID: `how-format`  \\nInput SHA: `ec045fadb47b598147052035b7eb1a9c47813156`  \\nVerification: HEAD equals the input SHA; `git show SHA:path` matched local bytes for all three assigned source files. Keep the finite-number guard, exact `toFixed(2)` conversion, string results and existing exports. Remove the private forwarding helper and unread formatter state only in a separately authorized change. This is a source-based recommendation. No candidate equivalence was executed.\\n\\nThe controller calculates an invoice total. Export formats supplied row totals through a separate path. The controller does not call the formatter. Sources at input revision `ec045fadb47b598147052035b7eb1a9c47813156`: `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/controller.mjs:1-2`, `facade.mjs:1-2`, `adapter.mjs:1-2`, `calculator.mjs:2-5`, `exporter.mjs:1-2`.\\n\\n## Key concepts\\n\\n`calculate(rows)` reduces `qty * unitPrice` into a JavaScript number. Its latest completed total remains observable through `inspectCachedTotal`. `formatRows(rows)` instead consumes `row.total`. Nothing in the inspected files connects these inputs. Sources: `calculator.mjs:1-6`, `formatter.mjs:8`.\\n\\n## How it works\\n\\n```mermaid\\nflowchart LR\\n    Controller --> Facade --> Adapter --> Calculator\\n    Calculator --> NumericTotal\\n    Exporter --> FormatRows --> FormatInvoice\\n    FormatInvoice --> FiniteCheck --> ToFixed2 --> Strings\\n```\\n\\n`formatInvoice` rejects nonfinite inputs with `Error('nonfinite')`. Otherwise `fixed` calls `value.toFixed(2)`, the result overwrites module-local `lastFormatted`, and the function returns it. `formatRows` maps row totals through this function. Export joins the strings with newlines. Sources: `formatter.mjs:1-8`, `exporter.mjs:1-2`.\\n\\nThe all-errors-swallowed premise is false within these files. No inspected function catches exceptions. Nonfinite formatter inputs explicitly throw. The controller can return `NaN` from unchecked arithmetic, but that is a value produced without an exception, not a swallowed exception. An outer application's catches remain unknown. Sources: `formatter.mjs:4`, `calculator.mjs:3`, `controller.mjs:2`.\\n\\n## Rounding motivation and confidence\\n\\n[Direct, synthetic scoped record] The supplied decision says a UI compares two-decimal strings and a CSV consumer imports `formatRows`. It explicitly places rounding at the display boundary. The fixture requires JavaScript binary floating-point `Number.toFixed(2)` behavior, not accounting decimal rules. Source: `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/decision.md:3,5`. This is fixture rationale, not verified customer history.\\n\\n[Inferred] Alternative A appears simpler to read because the retained formatter state has no exported reader and the helper forwards one operation. Source: `formatter.mjs:1-7`. This supports a recommendation, not a measured maintenance benefit or proven candidate equivalence.\\n\\n[Unknown] The original reason for `fixed` and `lastFormatted`, any actual customer requirement, historical incident and outer caller behavior remain unknown. The scoped Git history contains one seed commit. It establishes the fixture snapshot, not the original design history. No corroborated historical tier is claimed beyond the supplied synthetic record.\\n\\n| Alternative | Tradeoff | Failure mode |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| A removes `fixed` and `lastFormatted` | Fewer layers and no formatter state to track. Recommended from inspected requirements. | Accidentally removing validation, changing exports or replacing rounding semantics breaks the contract. Equivalence remains unexecuted. |\\n| B introduces a stateful `Formatter` class | Useful if a future requirement needs instance-owned state or configuration. | Adds construction and lifetime decisions. Replacing current function exports breaks imports. No such state requirement appears in scope. |\\n\\n## Where things live\\n\\nAll implementation citations above are within `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/`. `controller.mjs`, `facade.mjs` and `adapter.mjs` forward invoice input. `calculator.mjs` computes totals. `formatter.mjs` formats supplied totals. `exporter.mjs` emits newline-separated strings. `decision.md` supplies the synthetic contract and alternatives.\\n\\n## Gotchas\\n\\n`formatRows([])` returns an empty array by source reasoning. No example outputs were executed. A later invalid row aborts mapping after earlier rows may have updated private state. Calculator state has an exported reader, so removing formatter state does not justify deleting calculator state. Default lexical `sortAmounts` is a separately marked known bug. Sources: `formatter.mjs:4-10`, `calculator.mjs:6`.\\n\\n## Sources consulted\\n\\n- Source control history. One root seed commit and scoped blame, follow history and pattern search for `decision.md` and `formatter.mjs`, as returned by `/root/why_history`. The coordinator inspected the findings and verified all cited source bytes. No PR discussion was retrieved.\\n- Issue tracker. Denied by fixture-only scope. Not searched.\\n- Long-form documents. Local synthetic `decision.md` inspected. External documents denied and not searched.\\n- Team chat. Denied and not searched.\\n- Infrastructure observability. Denied and not searched.\\n\\nNo unavailable or denied external category is a searched-empty result. The authorized scoped history search found no separate incident rationale. Exact connector access was not tested because external services were prohibited.\\n\\n## Limits and future route\\n\\nIndependent explorers `/root/how_flow` and `/root/how_format`, explainer `/root/how_explainer`, investigator `/root/why_history` and synthesizer `/root/why_synthesizer` returned results at the correct input SHA. The coordinator checked citations before integration. Requested model and effort were inherited. Actual models and diversity remain unknown.\\n\\nThe how explainer's statement that no historical decision was supplied was too broad for the complete case. Other six categories denied by fixture-only authorization.\\\",\\n  \\\"promptPath\\\": \\\"/Users/digital-hawk/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.0/skills/why/references/investigator-prompt.md\\\",\\n  \\\"companions\\\": [\\n    \\\"/Users/digital-hawk/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.0/skills/why/references/epistemics.md\\\",\\n    \\\"/Users/digital-hawk/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.0/skills/why/references/sources/code-archaeology.md\\\",\\n    \\\"/Users/digital-hawk/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.0/skills/why/references/sources/incident-postmortem.md\\\"\\n  ]\\n}\\n\\nFull role prompt:\\n# Investigator Prompt Template\\n\\nBuild each investigator's prompt from this template. Fill in the placeholders. Append the single category playbook `sources/<source>.md` matching this investigator's evidence category (see `source-playbook.md` for the index). If the target code looks defensive (null checks, retry logic, timeout handling, rate limiting, feature flags, egress guards, OOM handlers), also append `sources/incident-postmortem.md` for the incident-flavored queries to run inside its own source.\\n\\n---\\n\\nYou are investigating the historical context and motivation behind a piece of code. A separate synthesizer combines your findings with other investigators' into a final answer, so gather evidence accurately rather than writing prose.\\n\\nOther investigators search different sources in parallel. Don't try to cover everything. Focus on your assigned source and go deep.\\n\\n## Operating Posture\\n\\nWork like a careful, cautious, precise investigator. Don't produce a narrative. Surface evidence and describe it accurately, including the parts that don't fit a tidy story. The more boring and exact your output, the more useful it is. A single verbatim quote with a precise citation beats a paragraph of plausible-sounding summary.\\n\\n- **Quote, don't paraphrase** when the exact wording matters. Citations should let the reader jump to the source and confirm the claim in seconds.\\n- **Go wide before going deep.** Cast a broad first net so you don't miss related context. Only then narrow in.\\n- **Track what you searched, not just what you found.** An absence is only useful if the reader knows what was looked for. Record queries verbatim.\\n- **Resist the story.** If three pieces of evidence line up neatly and a fourth contradicts them, the contradiction is the most interesting finding. Don't file it away.\\n- **Consider the counterfactual.** Before reporting a finding as strong, ask whether you would expect to find it if your current reading were wrong, and how the evidence would differ.\\n- **Never invent.** If you're tempted to round a partial finding up into a confident statement, stop and label it partial. The synthesizer is counting on your output being accurate.\\n\\n## The Question\\n\\n> {QUESTION}\\n\\n## The Code Anchor\\n\\n**Target files:** {FILES_WITH_LINE_RANGES}\\n\\n**Key symbols:** {SYMBOLS}\\n\\n**Initial commits touching this code (most recent first):**\\n{COMMIT_LIST}\\n\\n**PR numbers extracted from commit messages:** {PR_NUMBERS}\\n\\n**Ticket IDs mentioned in commits or PR bodies (if any):** {TICKET_IDS}\\n\\n## Your Assigned Source\\n\\n{SOURCE_NAME}\\n\\n{SOURCE_PLAYBOOK_SECTION}\\n\\n## Investigation Instructions\\n\\nGather **evidence**. Don't answer the question directly. The synthesizer weighs the evidence and forms conclusions. Follow this loop:\\n\\n1. **Cast a wide net first.** Start broad so you don't miss related context, then narrow in on specific items.\\n2. **Read the whole thing.** Read any PR, ticket, doc, or thread fully, not just the title or summary. The key evidence is often buried in a comment, a subtask, or a follow-up.\\n3. **Follow links within your assigned source.** If a PR references another PR or commit, pull it. If a ticket links a parent or sibling, pull it. If a doc links another doc, pull it. Stay inside your assigned source. When you spot a cross-source reference, do NOT chase it yourself. Record it under \\\"Additional Leads\\\" so the investigator assigned to that source can pick it up. The one-investigator-per-category design depends on this. Chasing cross-source links duplicates work and confuses scope.\\n4. **Capture quotes verbatim** with their location (PR number, ticket ID, URL, commit hash, file:line). The synthesizer needs to cite this precisely.\\n5. **Note absences.** If you searched for something and came up empty, that's also a finding. Record what you searched for and what you didn't find.\\n6. **Watch for contradictions.** If two items in your source disagree, record both. Don't suppress the inconvenient one.\\n\\nDon't synthesize or form a final opinion on \\\"the why.\\\" Collect the raw material honestly and completely. The synthesizer does the reasoning.\\n\\n## Epistemic Discipline\\n\\n- **Don't confuse mechanics with motivation.** A commit changing `limit = 50` to `limit = 100` shows the change, not necessarily why. Look for the explanation in the commit message, PR description, linked ticket, or review comments.\\n- **Don't infer intent from code style.** \\\"The author chose a functional approach\\\" is an observation about code, not evidence of intent. Claim intent only when the author stated it.\\n- **Preserve uncertainty.** If the evidence is ambiguous, say so. If one reading is more plausible but not certain, say that. Don't collapse ambiguity to look decisive.\\n- **No silent substitutions.** If the question is about feature X and you only find evidence about feature Y, don't present Y's evidence as if it answers X.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nReturn your findings in this structure. The synthesizer will read it directly.\\n\\n### Source\\nWhich source you investigated (source control, issue / ticket tracker, long-form documents, real-time team chat, infrastructure observability, error / exception tracking, product analytics warehouse, code comments, etc.).\\n\\n### What I Searched\\nThe queries you ran, the items you opened, the places you looked. Be specific. This tells the synthesizer how thorough the investigation was and what might still be unsearched.\\n\\n### Direct Evidence Found\\nFor each piece that explicitly addresses the question:\\n- **What it says**: verbatim quote or accurate paraphrase\\n- **Where it's from**: PR #123, ticket ID, doc URL, chat permalink, commit hash, or file:line\\n- **Author and date** (if available)\\n- **Relevance**: one sentence on how it bears on the question\\n\\n### Indirect / Circumstantial Evidence\\nItems that don't explicitly answer the question but bear on it. For each:\\n- **What it is**: brief description\\n- **Where it's from**: location\\n- **What it suggests**: what a careful reader might infer, and why. Name the inference chain.\\n- **Alternative readings**: if the same evidence could support a different interpretation, note it\\n\\n### Contradictions\\nTwo items that disagree with each other, with both citations.\\n\\n### Gaps\\nWhat you searched for and didn't find. Be specific: \\\"Searched the issue tracker for [query] across [time range]. No matching issues.\\\" These absences are valuable data.\\n\\n### Additional Leads\\nAnything that suggests further investigation in a different source. For example, if a PR references a chat thread that wasn't in your source, note it so the real-time team chat investigator or a follow-up pass can pursue it.\\n\\n## What You're Not Doing\\n\\n- Writing the final answer. The synthesizer does that.\\n- Picking sides in contradictions. Surface them.\\n- Speculating beyond what the evidence supports. A hunch with no evidence isn't evidence.\\n- Reading the code itself to figure out intent. You may read the code to understand what the target *is*, but don't confuse \\\"what the code does\\\" with \\\"why.\\\"\\n\\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/durable-03/read-only-answer/why-synthesizer.assignment.json\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-03/read-only-answer/why-synthesizer.assignment.json\",\n      \"sha256\": \"ee53cdbda47695c760f25447333981a6fd70e8c547106dd84c0e00eaeeb3bc63\",\n      \"bytes\": 1055,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-durable-03.json#0\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"{\\n  \\\"id\\\": \\\"why-synthesizer\\\",\\n  \\\"role\\\": \\\"why synthesizer\\\",\\n  \\\"inputRevision\\\": \\\"ec045fadb47b598147052035b7eb1a9c47813156\\\",\\n  \\\"outputOwner\\\": \\\"native worker pending identity\\\",\\n  \\\"isolatedOutputPath\\\": \\\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-03/read-only-answer/why-synthesizer.native-result.md\\\",\\n  \\\"retryCount\\\": 0,\\n  \\\"terminalStatus\\\": \\\"pending\\\",\\n  \\\"requestedModel\\\": \\\"inherit-parent\\\",\\n  \\\"requestedEffort\\\": \\\"inherit-parent\\\",\\n  \\\"actualModel\\\": \\\"unknown\\\",\\n  \\\"scope\\\": \\\"Read-only invoice explanation, rounding rationale, premise check and recommendation A vs B. Actual model/effort: unknown.\\\\n\\\\nthroughput checkpoint: n/a, read-only investigation.\\\\n\\\\n### The Question\\\\n\\\\nHow does the formatter execute, why does rounding occur at the display boundary, and does the code swallow all errors? Which supplied alternative is preferable: A removes `fixed` and `lastFormatted`; B introduces a stateful `Formatter` class?\\\\n\\\\n### The Code in Question\\\\n\\\\nAll citations refer to revision `ec045fadb47b598147052035b7eb1a9c47813156`.\\\\n\\\\n`committed-inputs/additional/invoice/formatter.mjs:1–8` contains `fixed`, `formatInvoice` and `formatRows`. The scoped rationale and contract appear in `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/decision.md:3–5`. The controller and export paths are separate.\\\\n\\\\n### What We Found\\\\n\\\\n- **[Direct]** The synthetic decision record explicitly places rounding at the display boundary because a UI consumer compares two-decimal strings and a CSV consumer imports `formatRows`. This establishes the fixture's rationale, not an independently verified customer requirement. Source: `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/decision.md:5`.\\\\n\\\\n- **[Direct]** The display contract requires JavaScript `Number.toFixed(2)`, including binary floating-point rounding behavior. Replacing it with accounting decimal rules would change that contract. Source: `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/decision.md:3`.\\\\n\\\\n- **[Direct]** `formatInvoice` rejects nonfinite inputs, calls `fixed`, assigns the resulting string to module-local `lastFormatted`, and returns it. `fixed` calls `value.toFixed(2)`. These are implementation mechanics, not evidence of historical intent. Source: `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/formatter.mjs:1–7`.\\\\n\\\\n- **[Direct]** `formatRows` maps each row's `total` through `formatInvoice`; the exporter joins those strings with newline separators. The contract specifies `[]` for empty row lists. Sources: `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/formatter.mjs:8`; `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/exporter.mjs:1–2`; `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/decision.md:3`.\\\\n\\\\n- **[Direct]** The blanket premise that all errors are swallowed is false within the inspected fixture. `formatInvoice` explicitly throws `Error('nonfinite')`, and neither that function nor `formatRows` catches it. The decision record also explicitly rejects the premise. Sources: `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/formatter.mjs:3–8`; `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/decision.md:5`.\\\\n\\\\n- **[Direct]** The controller is a separate calculation path. It calls the facade, then the adapter and calculator; it does not invoke the formatter. None of these inspected functions catches exceptions. Thus, \\\\\\\"the controller does not swallow errors\\\\\\\" must not be read as evidence that this controller receives formatter errors. Sources: `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/controller.mjs:1–2`; `facade.mjs:1–2`; `adapter.mjs:1–2`; `calculator.mjs:2–4`.\\\\n\\\\n- **[Direct]** Alternatives A and B are described, but their implementations are not supplied in the inspected decision record. The record asks for reader-cost and behavioral-equivalence comparison. Source: `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/decision.md:5`.\\\\n\\\\n### What We Can Reasonably Infer\\\\n\\\\n- **[Inferred]** Alternative A appears preferable for this fixture. `fixed` performs one operation, and each successful call overwrites `lastFormatted` immediately before returning it. Removing these layers would likely reduce the amount of code a reader must follow. Reasoning: `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/formatter.mjs:1–7`.\\\\n\\\\n| Alternative | Likely reader cost | Equivalence conditions and risks |\\\\n|---|---|---|\\\\n| A: remove `fixed` and `lastFormatted` | Lower: validation and string conversion can remain together. | Preserve the finite-number guard, exact `toFixed(2)` behavior, returned strings, exports and `formatRows` behavior. |\\\\n| B: stateful `Formatter` class | Higher: readers must track construction, instance ownership and state. | Preserve the same contract and establish whether state changes observable behavior. No requirement for persistent formatter state surfaced. |\\\\n\\\\n- **[Inferred]** A could preserve the current observable formatting behavior if it retains those conditions. This is a source-based assessment, not verified equivalence: no candidate implementation was inspected or executed.\\\\n\\\\n### What We Don't Know\\\\n\\\\n- Why the original author introduced `fixed` or `lastFormatted`. The investigator found only the root seed commit, titled “Seed durable workflow evaluation inputs,” across both scoped histories.\\\\n- Whether a real UI or customer ever required these exact comparisons. The record explicitly says no customer statement or external ticket was retrieved.\\\\n- Whether an outer application catches errors beyond the inspected fixture paths.\\\\n- Whether A or B actually preserves behavior. No implementation, build, prototype or runtime check occurred.\\\\n- The investigator's scoped history search for `formatInvoice|fixed|formatRows|incident|defensive|revert` found no separate incident, defensive-check or revert rationale. This absence applies only to the inspected fixture paths.\\\\n- Original authors, consumer owners or product owners might supply missing context, but no external contact was authorized.\\\\n\\\\n### Sources Consulted\\\\n\\\\n- **Source control history**: Inspected the investigator's complete findings for `decision.md` and `formatter.mjs`, covering one root commit, complete blame, file-follow history and the scoped pattern search. Independently confirmed HEAD and spot-checked committed source citations. No PR discussion was available in that evidence.\\\\n- **Issue / ticket tracker**: Not searched. External tracker access was denied by fixture-only authorization.\\\\n- **Long-form documents**: Read the local synthetic `decision.md`. External documents were not searched because authorization excluded them.\\\\n- **Real-time team chat**: Not searched. External chat access was denied by scope.\\\\n- **Infrastructure observability**: Not searched. External logs, traces, metrics and incidents were denied by scope.\\\\n- **Error / exception tracking**: Not searched. External exception records were denied by scope.\\\\n- **Product analytics warehouse**: Not searched. External warehouse queries were denied by scope.\\\\n\\\\n### Confidence Summary\\\\n\\\\nThe fixture's rounding rationale and formatting mechanics have direct evidence. The all-errors-swallowed premise conflicts with both the explicit throw and the synthetic record, with the qualification that the controller follows a separate calculation path. Alternative A is the stronger source-based recommendation; original production motivation and candidate equivalence remain unverified.\\\\n\\\"\\n}\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"path\": \"fixtures/durable-03/read-only-answer/why-synthesizer.native-result.md\",\n      \"originalExternalPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-03/read-only-answer/why-synthesizer.native-result.md\",\n      \"sha256\": \"36457ffd4fa058f3db8eb810de320127c14402384355f6cc1036dc6f004de6f1\",\n      \"bytes\": 6765,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-durable-03.json#0\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"Assignment: `why-synthesizer`  \\nInput revision: `ec045fadb47b598147052035b7eb1a9c47813156`  \\nRequested model/effort: inherit-parent. Fill in the placeholders.\\n\\n---\\n\\nYou are answering a \\\"why\\\" question about a piece of code by synthesizing findings from multiple investigators who searched different historical sources (source control, issue / ticket tracker, long-form documents, real-time team chat, infrastructure observability, error / exception tracking, product analytics warehouse, and code comments). Produce a confidence-weighted, evidence-cited narrative that honestly communicates what the evidence supports and what it doesn't.\\n\\n## The Question\\n\\n> {QUESTION}\\n\\n## The Code Anchor\\n\\n**Target files:** {FILES_WITH_LINE_RANGES}\\n\\n**Key symbols:** {SYMBOLS}\\n\\n## Investigator Findings\\n\\n{ALL_INVESTIGATOR_FINDINGS}\\n\\n## Sources That Weren't Searched\\n\\n{SKIPPED_SOURCES_WITH_REASONS}\\n\\n## Epistemics Framework\\n\\nYou MUST follow the framework in `references/epistemics.md`. Read it in full before writing the output. The key rules:\\n\\n1. Every claim sits in one of these tiers: **Direct**, **Supported**, **Inferred**, **Speculative**, **Unknown**. The tier determines what section the claim goes in and how it's phrased.\\n2. Every Direct/Supported claim must have a citation (PR #, ticket ID, doc URL, chat permalink, commit hash, or file:line).\\n3. Inferred and Speculative claims must use hedged language (\\\"appears to\\\", \\\"likely\\\", \\\"suggests\\\", \\\"one possibility is\\\").\\n4. Never cite code as evidence for its own intent.\\n5. Gaps in the evidence must be documented. Don't fill them with plausible-sounding guesses.\\n6. If the user's question embedded a hypothesis, treat it as a candidate, not a conclusion. Check the evidence independently.\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\n1. **Read all investigator findings.** They gathered raw evidence, not conclusions. You weigh it.\\n2. **Reconcile overlapping findings.** Multiple investigators may have cited the same PR, ticket, or doc. Merge into a single, authoritative reference.\\n3. **Identify contradictions.** If two items of evidence disagree, don't pick one. Surface both.\\n4. **Calibrate confidence.** For each claim, identify the evidence and the tier. State Direct claims plainly with a citation. Hedge Inferred claims and explain the inference. Mark Speculative claims explicitly. Put claims with no evidence in the gaps section.\\n5. **Verify citations by spot-checking.** You can read the codebase and call MCP tools to verify citations. Do not write files, commit, or modify external state. If you're uncertain a cited item exists or says what's claimed, check it. Don't propagate errors.\\n6. **Don't overreach.** The user will act on your output. Better to leave an open question open than to fill it with a confident-sounding guess.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nWrite the output for the user. Use this exact structure:\\n\\n---\\n\\n### The Question\\n\\nRestate the user's question in one or two sentences so the answer is anchored.\\n\\n### The Code in Question\\n\\nFile paths, line ranges, key symbols. Two or three lines to orient a reader who lands here cold.\\n\\n### What We Found\\n\\n**Claims with direct evidence**, one per bullet. Quote or paraphrase the source and cite precisely. Format each finding like:\\n\\n```markdown\\n- **[Direct]** {Claim}. Source: [PR #123](url) / ticket ID / file:line. {Brief quote or paraphrase.}\\n- **[Supported]** {Claim}. Evidence: {list of items and what each contributes}.\\n```\\n\\nUse `[Direct]` for single-source, explicit evidence. Use `[Supported]` when multiple indirect items converge on a conclusion.\\n\\n### What We Can Reasonably Infer\\n\\n**Claims that aren't explicitly stated anywhere but are well-supported by indirect evidence.** Make the inference chain visible: \\\"Given A and B, it's likely that C.\\\" Use hedged language (\\\"appears to\\\", \\\"likely\\\", \\\"suggests\\\", \\\"is consistent with\\\"). Format:\\n\\n- **[Inferred]** {Hedged claim}. Reasoning: {the specific evidence and the inference step}.\\n\\nIf there's nothing to infer, skip this section.\\n\\n### Competing Hypotheses\\n\\n**If the evidence fits multiple stories, present them.** Don't force a winner when the record doesn't support one. For each hypothesis:\\n\\n- **Hypothesis:** {one-sentence statement}\\n- **Evidence for:** {specific items}\\n- **Evidence against or missing:** {what would need to be true but isn't, or what counter-signals exist}\\n\\nSkip this section if there's a single clear answer.\\n\\n### What We Don't Know\\n\\n**Explicit gaps.** Things the user asked that the evidence didn't answer. Sources searched that came up empty. Sources that weren't searchable at all, such as a missing real-time team chat MCP.\\n\\nBe specific. \\\"We searched the issue tracker for [query1], [query2], [query3] and found no issue discussing the rate-limit threshold\\\" is useful. \\\"We don't know why\\\" is not. Format:\\n\\n- **Source control history**: {file paths}, {number of commits reviewed}, PRs #{numbers}, and code comments searched. Or \\\"Not searched. Git history, command execution or authenticated PR access was unavailable or denied; name the exact gap.\\\"\\n- **Issue / ticket tracker**: {ticket IDs and keyword searches}. Or \\\"Not searched. No matching MCP available in this environment.\\\"\\n- **Real-time team chat**: {channels searched, date ranges, queries}. Or \\\"Not searched. No matching MCP available in this environment.\\\"\\n- **Infrastructure observability**: {dashboards, monitors, metrics, logs, traces, or incidents searched}. Or \\\"Not searched. No matching MCP available in this environment.\\\"\\n- **Error / exception tracking**: {issues, events, or releases searched}. Or \\\"Not searched. No matching MCP available in this environment.\\\"\\n- **Product analytics warehouse**: {fully-qualified tables queried, the time windows, and the numeric summaries (counts, percentiles, first/last-seen timestamps) that bore on the question}. Or \\\"Not searched. No matching MCP available in this environment.\\\"\\n\\n### Confidence Summary\\n\\nOne or two sentences summarizing your overall confidence. E.g.:\\n\\n> \\\"The core rationale (A) is well-supported by direct PR and ticket evidence. The specific threshold value (100) is inferred from the surrounding context but not explicitly documented. The question of whether this was driven by a customer request could not be answered. No relevant issue tracker or long-form doc content surfaced, and real-time team chat search was unavailable.\\\"\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Quality Check Before Returning\\n\\nBefore finalizing, review your output against this checklist:\\n\\n1. Does every claim in \\\"What We Found\\\" have a citation? If not, add one or move the claim to \\\"Inferred\\\" or \\\"Hypotheses.\\\"\\n2. Is the phrasing tier-appropriate? Does the \\\"What We Don't Know\\\" section exist and name specific gaps? If it's empty or missing, be suspicious. Historical investigations almost always have gaps.\\n5. If the user embedded a hypothesis in their question, did you check it against the evidence rather than rubber-stamping it?\\n6. Did you cite any code as evidence for its own intent? Remove those. Code is mechanics, not motivation.\\n7. Is the overall tone calibrated? A confident-sounding answer with weak evidence is the exact failure mode this skill exists to prevent.\\n\\nIf any item fails, revise before returning.\\n\\n## A Final Note\\n\\nThe value of this output comes from its honesty, not its authority. The controller calls facade, adapter and calculator to return `{ total }`; export separately calls `formatRows`, then `formatInvoice`. Sources: `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/controller.mjs:1-2`, `facade.mjs:1-2`, `adapter.mjs:1-2`, `calculator.mjs:2-5`, `exporter.mjs:1-2`.\\n\\n## How it works\\n\\nThe formatter requires `Number.isFinite(value)`, throws `Error('nonfinite')` otherwise, converts valid numbers with `toFixed(2)` and returns the string through private `lastFormatted`. It requires the existing binary floating-point conversion. Source: `decision.md:3,5`.\\n\\n[Inferred] A appears to reduce reader cost because no external consumer reads `lastFormatted`. This is not executed equivalence. Source: `formatter.mjs:1-7`.\\n\\n| Alternative | Benefit | Tradeoff |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| A removes helper and private state | Fewer layers and less state. Recommended conditionally. | Must preserve finite check, exact conversion, string results, exports and row behavior. |\\n| B adds stateful class | Could isolate future instance-specific configuration. | Adds instance lifetime and ownership with no requirement supplied. |\\n\\n## Where things live and gotchas\\n\\nAll cited files are in `committed-inputs/additional/invoice/`. Calculator state remains observable through `inspectCachedTotal` and must be preserved. The separate lexical sorting defect stays out of scope. Sources: `calculator.mjs:6`, `formatter.mjs:9-10`.\\n\\n## Sources consulted and unknowns\\n\\nSource control category uses only committed fixture bytes in this variant; no additional history search occurred.       "text": "# read-only-answer\n\nInput revision e7fbeceb20469acf0c73a0e946f85e3ce51d0b37 verified before dispatch and independently by all three workers. Package 0.1.3 manifest observed. The earlier generic assignment.json was not used for these routed workers.\n\n## Actual explanation and recommendation\n\n### Overview\n\nformatInvoice rejects nonfinite inputs and returns a string from Number.toFixed(2). formatRows applies it to each row's total. The premise that all errors are swallowed is false within this module. The formatter explicitly throws and neither function catches errors. Source committed-inputs/shared/additional/invoice/formatter.mjs:3-8.\n\nRecommend alternative A, removing fixed and lastFormatted while preserving validation and returned strings. This module exposes no state reader, and the helper adds no policy beyond toFixed(2).\n\n### Key Concepts\n\nfixed(value) calls value.toFixed(2). lastFormatted is module-local mutable state. Each successful call overwrites it and returns its value. formatRows(rows) maps row totals through formatInvoice. Source formatter.mjs:1-8.\n\n### How It Works\n\nA caller invokes formatInvoice(value). Number.isFinite(value) runs first. If it returns false, the function throws Error('nonfinite') before formatting or assigning state. Otherwise, fixed produces the string, lastFormatted receives it, and the function returns it. Source formatter.mjs:3-7.\n\nformatRows maps each row.total through that flow. An empty array produces an empty array. A formatter exception propagates through map; this invocation returns no completed result array. There is no catch in this module. Source formatter.mjs:8.\n\nThe synthetic decision record says a UI compares two-decimal strings and a CSV consumer imports formatRows. It records those consumers as the reason rounding belongs at the display boundary. That supports the fixture's stated rationale, not a claim about real project history. The contract preserves JavaScript binary floating-point behavior rather than substituting accounting decimal rules. Sources decision.md:3,5.\n\n| Alternative | Reader cost | Behavioral equivalence and tradeoffs |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| A removes fixed and lastFormatted | One function contains validation and formatting; fewer names and assignments to trace. | Can preserve exports, guard, strings and exceptions. Removes state with no shown reader. Best fit for this module. |\n| B introduces stateful Formatter class | Adds construction, instance ownership and mutable-state lifecycle. | Can preserve formatting results, but caller compatibility depends on public interface. Useful if consumers require instances or observable state; neither appears in inspected source. |\n\nThese are proposals named in decision.md:5. Neither alternative was implemented or executed, so equivalence remains unproved.\n\n### Where Things Live\n\nBoth files live under committed-inputs/shared/additional/invoice. formatter.mjs contains formatter, row adapter and unrelated sorting helper. decision.md contains synthetic contract, rationale and alternative descriptions.\n\n### Gotchas\n\nsortAmounts uses toSorted() without a numeric comparator. The lexical defect is explicitly separate from this refactor, formatter.mjs:9-10 and decision.md:7. The controller statement is supported only by the synthetic record; controller source was outside the two-file slice. Runtime behavior was not executed. No outputs for 0, 1.005, -1.005, 12.345, empty rows, NaN or Infinity were observed.\n\n## Actual why synthesis\n\n### The Question\n\nWhy does invoice rounding belong at the display boundary? Does the inspected path support the premise that all errors are swallowed?\n\n### The Code in Question\n\nAt revision e7fbeceb20469acf0c73a0e946f85e3ce51d0b37, committed-inputs/shared/additional/invoice/formatter.mjs:1-10 contains fixed, formatInvoice, formatRows and sortAmounts. The rationale and preserved behavior appear in committed-inputs/shared/additional/invoice/decision.md:3-7.\n\n### What We Found\n\n- [Direct] The synthetic decision record explicitly places rounding at the display boundary because a UI consumer compares two-decimal strings and a CSV consumer imports formatRows. This is documented fixture rationale, without independently inspected consumer evidence.       "text": "Producing worker /root/why_synthesis. Capturing owner /root. Lightly edited native response capture; exact native full return remains in transcript.\n\n### The Question\n\nWhy does invoice rounding belong at the display boundary? Does the inspected path support the premise that all errors are swallowed?\n\n### The Code in Question\n\nAt revision e7fbeceb20469acf0c73a0e946f85e3ce51d0b37, committed-inputs/shared/additional/invoice/formatter.mjs:1-10 contains fixed, formatInvoice, formatRows and sortAmounts. The rationale and preserved behavior appear in committed-inputs/shared/additional/invoice/decision.md:3-7.\n\n### What We Found\n\n- [Direct] The synthetic decision record explicitly places rounding at the display boundary because a UI consumer compares two-decimal strings and a CSV consumer imports formatRows. This is documented fixture rationale, without independently inspected consumer evidence. Source decision.md:5.\n- [Direct] The display contract requires JavaScript Number.toFixed(2), including binary floating-point behavior. It also requires empty rows to produce [] and nonfinite values to throw Error('nonfinite'). Source decision.md:3.\n- [Direct, mechanics] The universal all-errors-swallowed premise conflicts with the inspected formatter path. formatInvoice explicitly throws for nonfinite values, and formatRows calls it without a catch. These lines establish local mechanics, not behavior of every consumer. Source formatter.mjs:4-8.\n- [Direct] The decision record additionally states that the controller does not swallow errors. The controller was not inspected, so this remains a documented claim rather than independently verified runtime behavior. Source decision.md:5.\n- [Direct] The record identifies two proposed alternatives and asks for reader cost and behavioral equivalence; it does not document a winning implementation. Source decision.md:5.\n- [Direct] The lexical sorting defect is explicitly unrelated. Sources formatter.mjs:9-10, decision.md:7.\n\n### What We Can Reasonably Infer\n\n- [Inferred] Replacing toFixed(2) with accounting decimal rounding would likely violate the intended compatibility boundary. The record explicitly preserves JavaScript rounding behavior and identifies consumers that depend on formatted output. Source decision.md:3-5.\n- [Inferred] A cleanup can likely remove incidental formatter state while retaining the documented output contract, but equivalence has not been demonstrated here. No alternative was implemented or executed. Sources formatter.mjs:1-8, decision.md:5.\n\n### What We Don't Know\n\n- [Unknown] Whether documented UI, CSV and controller behavior matches implementation. Only two assigned fixture files were inspected.\n- [Unknown] Any external customer requirement, ticket, incident, original author or decision date. Synthetic record reports no retrieved customer statement or external ticket.\n- [Unknown] Edge-case runtime outputs and behavioral equivalence of either alternative. Product execution, tests and edits were outside assignment.\n- [Unknown] External evidence across six other categories. Those sources were forbidden, not searched-empty.\n\n### Sources Consulted\n\n- Source control history: both assigned fixture files reread and HEAD verified. Coordinator inspected one seed commit, Seed isolated workflow inputs, without PR/ticket lineage. Synthesizer did not independently inspect history.\n- Issue / ticket tracker: not searched, external reads forbidden.\n- Long-form documents: external search forbidden; in-repository synthetic decision.md inspected.\n- Real-time team chat: not searched, external reads forbidden.\n- Infrastructure observability: not searched, external reads forbidden.\n- Error / exception tracking: not searched, external reads forbidden.\n- Product analytics warehouse: not searched, external reads forbidden.\n\n### Confidence Summary\n\nThe display-boundary rationale has Direct support in the synthetic decision record. The inspected formatter contradicts the universal swallowed-error premise locally. Confidence about real consumer behavior and external historical motivation remains limited because those sources were not inspected.\n\n"
