    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/read-only-answer/outputs/raw/root-sources-d.json",
      "bytes": 16625,
      "sha256": "83ee5e41beec820c7461346cfa9b67e8597ad3d531c5a82f71204b7bd34d4400",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-read-only-answer",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\"args\":{\"cmd\":\"cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/references/sources/datadog.md'; cat '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/references/epistemics.md'; sed -n '130,280p' '/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/SKILL.md'\",\"max_output_tokens\":6500},\"result\":{\"chunk_id\":\"946922\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.00000625,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":3879,\"output\":\"# Datadog Telemetry\\n\\n## What this source contains\\n\\nDatadog holds the runtime record, what actually happened in production, as opposed to what was planned or discussed.\\n\\n- **Metrics.** Counters, gauges, histograms instrumented by the team. A metric's *presence* is itself evidence. Someone thought this number worth watching.\\n- **Monitors & alerts.** Conditions the team decided warranted waking someone up. A monitor firing on `rate_limit_hit > 10/min` is direct evidence the team worried about that threshold.\\n- **Dashboards.** Curated views. The charts tell you what the team considers important for a subsystem.\\n- **APM traces & spans.** Request-level runtime data. Useful for \\\"why is this slow\\\" / \\\"why is there a timeout here\\\" questions.\\n- **Logs.** High-volume event records. Often contain the error conditions that motivated defensive code.\\n- **Incidents.** Formal incident records with timelines and linked postmortems.\\n- **Notebooks.** Exploratory investigations. Often contain hypotheses and analyses.\\n\\nDatadog answers \\\"what was the production reality around the time this code was written?\\\", which often explains the code's shape.\\n\\n## How to search it\\n\\nUse the Datadog MCP. Start broad, then narrow.\\n\\n1. **Identify the owning service(s).**\\n\\n   ```\\n   search_datadog_services (filter by name or team)\\n   search_datadog_service_dependencies (see upstream/downstream)\\n   ```\\n\\n2. **Dashboards and monitors first. They tell you what the team cares about.**\\n\\n   ```\\n   search_datadog_dashboards (query: feature name, service name, symbol)\\n   search_datadog_monitors   (same queries)\\n   ```\\n\\n   When a dashboard or monitor covers the target, note its queries and watched thresholds. The threshold is frequently the answer to \\\"why is this clamped at N?\\\"\\n\\n3. **Metrics around the target.**\\n\\n   ```\\n   search_datadog_metrics (by name pattern, e.g., the feature or symbol)\\n   get_datadog_metric_context (metadata: description, units, tags)\\n   get_datadog_metric (timeseries; \\\"was there a spike around the PR date?\\\")\\n   ```\\n\\n   Correlating a metric's trajectory with the target's add/change date is strong supporting evidence: \\\"the `payment_timeout` metric spiked 2023-11-03, and the retry logic merged 2023-11-06.\\\"\\n\\n4. **Logs. Narrow, don't dump.**\\n\\n   ```\\n   search_datadog_logs (raw log patterns near the target, set use_log_patterns=true)\\n   analyze_datadog_logs (SQL-style aggregations, only when you need counts)\\n   ```\\n\\n   Search with symbols, error strings, or feature names. **Strongly prefer time-bounded queries** (e.g., 30 days before/after the change). Log volume is huge. Unconstrained searches waste time and may time out.\\n\\n5. **APM spans and traces.**\\n\\n   ```\\n   aggregate_spans    (stats: \\\"how often does this endpoint fail?\\\")\\n   search_datadog_spans (inspect individual spans)\\n   get_datadog_trace  (a specific trace ID)\\n   ```\\n\\n   Useful for timeouts, retries, slow paths, and cross-service behavior.\\n\\n6. **Incidents.**\\n\\n   ```\\n   search_datadog_incidents (by title, team, date range)\\n   get_datadog_incident     (full detail for a specific incident)\\n   ```\\n\\n   If the target looks defensive, search for incidents around the time it was added. An incident whose timeline includes \\\"added defensive check for X\\\" is near-direct evidence.\\n\\n## What good evidence looks like here\\n\\n- A monitor whose query and threshold match the constraint the code enforces (code clamps to 100, monitor alerts when requests exceed 100/min)\\n- A dashboard created by the target's author, with widgets that correspond to what the code measures or guards against\\n- A metric showing a production spike immediately before the code was merged, and stable values after\\n- An incident record referencing the target code, the same symbols, or the same error strings\\n- Logs showing a specific error pattern the defensive code would prevent, timestamped in the window before the change\\n\\n## Common pitfalls\\n\\n- **Correlation is not causation.** A spike before a PR and stabilization after is suggestive, not definitive. Other changes may have landed in the same window. Check neighboring PRs.\\n- **Overfitting to the chart you found.** Datadog visualizations are *made* by humans and reflect that human's framing. A chart named \\\"retry success rate\\\" is evidence the team cared about retry success, not that it's why a specific line of code exists.\\n- **Vanished telemetry.** Metrics can be renamed, deleted, or have short retention. If you can't find data from the relevant window, that's a gap, not a null result.\\n- **Noise at scale.** Searching logs for a common string returns thousands of matches. Narrow by service, tag, and time aggressively. Use `analyze_datadog_logs` to aggregate rather than dumping raw logs.\\n- **Instrumented != caused.** A metric's existence tells you someone cared enough to measure something, not that the code was added *because* of it. Cross-reference with commit/PR dates.\\n\\n## What to return\\n\\nFor each relevant item:\\n- Type (dashboard / monitor / metric / log pattern / trace / incident / notebook)\\n- Title or name\\n- Link or identifier (dashboard ID, monitor ID, metric name, incident ID)\\n- Owner/author and created/modified date\\n- The specific condition, query, or quote that bears on the question (verbatim where possible)\\n- Relevance: what this suggests about the target code, and how strong the connection is\\n\\n## Session binding and access\\n\\nThese tool names and command examples describe source-specific operations, not guaranteed APIs. Inspect exposed schemas and bind equivalent authorized read operations before use. If execution, authentication, retention or source access is missing, record the precise gap. Do not install tools, change credentials, send messages or modify source state. Respect private-record visibility and return only relevant authorized excerpts or compact aggregates.\\n# Epistemics\\n\\nHow to reason about confidence when evidence is historical, fragmentary, and sometimes contradictory, and how to communicate it without flattening it into false certainty.\\n\\nCode doesn't carry its own motivation. You can read what code does. You can't read *why it exists*. That lives in commits, PRs, tickets, docs, and conversations, all incomplete, biased, and sometimes missing entirely. Pretending otherwise produces confident-sounding guesses that mislead the user.\\n\\n## Confidence Tiers\\n\\nEvery claim in the final output must sit in one of these tiers. The tier determines which output section the claim goes in and how it's phrased.\\n\\n### 1. Direct\\n\\nAn explicit, textual citation that answers the question. Not \\\"the code does X so the author must have wanted X.\\\" Something an author actually *wrote* that says why.\\n\\nExamples:\\n- A PR description that says \\\"this fixes the bug where users with >1000 items couldn't paginate\\\"\\n- A ticket that says \\\"we're adding this because customer Acme requested it in their security review\\\"\\n- A code comment that says \\\"// clamp to 100 because the upstream API rejects larger values\\\"\\n- A design doc that says \\\"we chose option A over option B because we need persistence across restarts\\\"\\n- A chat message from the author saying \\\"switching to this approach since the old one was flaky in tests\\\"\\n\\nPhrasing: confident, present tense. \\\"This exists because X.\\\" Cite the source.\\n\\n### 2. Supported\\n\\nMultiple pieces of indirect evidence converge. No single source states it explicitly, but the pattern across sources makes it likely.\\n\\nExamples:\\n- The PR title says \\\"improve performance,\\\" the ticket is labeled \\\"perf,\\\" and the surrounding commits all touch the same hot path\\n- Multiple tests were added alongside the change, all exercising edge cases with very large inputs\\n- The author's other PRs from the same week all mention the same incident in their descriptions\\n\\nPhrasing: confident but clearly derived. \\\"The evidence points strongly to X: [the specific pieces].\\\" Cite multiple sources.\\n\\n### 3. Inferred\\n\\nA reasonable reading of the context, but nothing explicitly supports it. The reader should understand this is *your interpretation*, not a fact from the record.\\n\\nExamples:\\n- The PR doesn't say why, but given the error was happening in production (per the incident channel timing) and the fix was rushed (merged the same day), it was likely a hotfix.\\n- The function name suggests retry logic. The retry count is 3. This matches the team's general convention of \\\"3 retries\\\" seen elsewhere in the codebase.\\n\\nPhrasing: hedged. \\\"It appears\\\", \\\"likely\\\", \\\"suggests\\\", \\\"is consistent with\\\", \\\"one reading is\\\". Make the inference chain explicit: \\\"Given A and B, C seems likely because D.\\\"\\n\\n### 4. Speculative\\n\\nA plausible hypothesis, but the evidence is thin and other explanations fit equally well. Presenting these is valuable, but mark them clearly as guesses.\\n\\nExamples:\\n- \\\"This might be a workaround for a browser bug that's since been fixed, but we found no contemporary evidence of that.\\\"\\n- \\\"It's possible this threshold was chosen to match an SLA commitment, but no SLA doc references it.\\\"\\n\\nPhrasing: explicitly speculative. \\\"One possibility is X, but we have no direct evidence.\\\" Usually lives in the \\\"Competing Hypotheses\\\" section alongside other possibilities.\\n\\n### 5. Unknown\\n\\nYou looked and couldn't find out. A valid and important outcome. Document it.\\n\\nPhrasing: \\\"We searched X, Y, and Z and found no evidence of why.\\\" Be specific about *what* you searched. \\\"We couldn't find out\\\" is less useful than \\\"we searched the ticket tracker with keywords A and B, scanned the 6 PRs that touched this file since 2023, and grep'd the repo for string literals matching the threshold. None surfaced a rationale.\\\"\\n\\n## Phrasing Guide\\n\\n### Words that carry confidence. Use carefully\\n\\nThese imply **Direct** or **Supported** confidence. Don't use them for inferences.\\n\\n- \\\"because\\\". Implies a causal claim with evidence\\n- \\\"the reason is\\\". Same\\n- \\\"was designed to\\\". Claims author intent\\n- \\\"fixes\\\", \\\"addresses\\\", \\\"solves\\\". Claims the change achieved its goal\\n- \\\"the team decided\\\". Claims a group decision happened\\n\\nIf you're using these, you should have a citation immediately adjacent.\\n\\n### Words that hedge. Use for inferences\\n\\n- \\\"appears to\\\"\\n- \\\"seems to\\\"\\n- \\\"likely\\\"\\n- \\\"suggests\\\"\\n- \\\"is consistent with\\\"\\n- \\\"one reading is\\\"\\n- \\\"plausibly\\\"\\n- \\\"may have been\\\"\\n- \\\"the evidence points toward\\\"\\n\\nThese signal that you're interpreting, not reporting. Use them liberally in the \\\"What We Can Reasonably Infer\\\" section.\\n\\n### Words to avoid\\n\\n- \\\"obviously\\\". If it were obvious, the user wouldn't be asking\\n- \\\"clearly\\\". Almost always precedes a claim that isn't clear\\n- \\\"of course\\\". Same\\n- \\\"just\\\" (as in \\\"it's just X for performance\\\"). Dismissive and usually hides uncertainty\\n- \\\"I think\\\" / \\\"I believe\\\". You're synthesizing evidence, not giving a personal opinion. Use \\\"the evidence suggests\\\" instead.\\n\\n### Avoid rationalization\\n\\nCode that \\\"makes sense\\\" today may have been written for reasons that no longer apply, or that were wrong when they were written. Don't retrofit a clean rationale onto messy history.\\n\\nResist the urge to:\\n- Assume the author did the \\\"right\\\" thing and work backward to justify it\\n- Assume a consistent pattern across the codebase was intentional when it might be copy-paste\\n- Turn an absence of evidence into evidence of absence (\\\"no one mentioned security concerns, so it must not have been a concern\\\")\\n\\n## The Sycophancy Trap\\n\\nUsers often phrase `why` questions with an embedded hypothesis: \\\"Why do we do it this way, I assume it's for performance?\\\" Don't simply confirm it. Treat it as one candidate among others and check the evidence independently. If the evidence supports it, say so with citations. If not, say so and present what the evidence *does* support.\\n\\nThe user's guess is a prompt for investigation, not a conclusion to validate.\\n\\n## When Evidence Contradicts\\n\\nIf two sources disagree (the PR description says one thing, the ticket says another), surface both. Don't pick the one that fits a tidier narrative. A typical pattern:\\n\\n- **The ticket says** \\\"we need this for customer X's compliance requirement\\\"\\n- **The PR says** \\\"cleaning up tech debt in this area\\\"\\n\\nBoth may be true (the ticket motivated the work, the PR is the author's framing of it), or one may be wrong. Present both with their citations and let the user make the call.\\n\\n## When Evidence Is Missing\\n\\nAn honest \\\"we don't know\\\" is one of the most valuable outputs this skill can produce. The user now knows:\\n\\n- The answer isn't in the obvious places\\n- They'll need to ask a human (the original author, the product owner, the team lead) to find out\\n- Or they can decide the question isn't worth pursuing further\\n\\nFailing to mark a gap and filling it with a confident guess actively harms the user. They'll act on the guess.\\n\\nWhen you hit a gap, name it concretely:\\n- What question you were trying to answer\\n- What sources you searched\\n- What you searched for in each\\n- What you found (nothing, or only tangentially related material)\\n\\n## Calibration Check Before Finalizing\\n\\nBefore delivering the output, the synthesizer should review every claim in \\\"What We Found\\\" and \\\"What We Can Reasonably Infer\\\" and ask:\\n\\n1. Does this claim have a citation? If not, either add one or move it to \\\"Inferred\\\" / \\\"Hypotheses\\\".\\n2. Is the phrasing calibrated to the tier? (A Direct claim can use \\\"because\\\". An Inferred claim cannot.)\\n3. Am I treating the code itself as evidence for its own intent? If so, that's not evidence. Remove or reclassify.\\n4. Does the output include a \\\"What We Don't Know\\\" section? If no gaps are mentioned, that's suspicious. Either the evidence was unusually complete or something is being swept under the rug.\\n\\n## Step 5. Present\\n\\nTake the synthesizer's output and present it to the user. You may lightly edit for clarity or add context from the conversation, but **do not rewrite the confidence language**.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nThe output structure is the one in `references/synthesizer-prompt.md`: The Question, The Code in Question, What We Found, What We Can Reasonably Infer, Competing Hypotheses, What We Don't Know, Sources Consulted, Confidence Summary. Adapt as needed, but keep the confidence separation intact, and keep Sources Consulted as one line per investigator, including the ones that returned nothing or were skipped, with the reason.\\n\\nAfter the Sources Consulted block, if the user's `why` question is a precursor to actually changing this code, convert the lineage findings into a Preserve / Change / Avoid / Risk constraint set suitable for planning the change.\\n\\n## Common Failure Modes to Avoid\\n\\n- **Recency bias**. Assuming the most recent commit is authoritative. The current shape is often the accretion of many earlier decisions. Trace back.\\n\\nKeep private source visibility intact. Retrieve only authorized relevant content, avoid raw telemetry or personal-data dumps, and do not redistribute restricted records beyond the authorized audience. External messages and writes require separate explicit authorization.\\n\\n## Reference Files\\n\\n- `references/epistemics.md`. Confidence tiers and phrasing guide. The synthesizer must follow it.\\n- `references/investigator-prompt.md`. Base prompt template for investigator subagents.\\n- `references/source-playbook.md`. Index pointing at the category playbooks below.\\n- `references/sources/*.md`. One self-contained example playbook per category, plus cross-cutting `incident-postmortem.md`. Give an investigator the single file that matches its category and adapt it to the available MCP.\\n- `references/synthesizer-prompt.md`. Prompt template for the synthesizer subagent, including the output format.\\n\"}}\n"
    },

Do not silently replace this with accounting decimal rules. Empty row lists produce []. Nonfinite amounts throw Error('nonfinite'). Capture observed outputs before edits for 0, 1.005, -1.005, 12.345, empty rows, NaN and Infinity. Output examples in a test are expectations, not proof a test ran.\\n     4\\t\\n     5\\tFixture history decision: a UI consumer compares two-decimal strings and a CSV consumer imports formatRows. This is why rounding belongs at the display boundary. No customer statement or external ticket was retrieved. Errors are thrown by formatInvoice and are not swallowed by the controller. Compare reader cost and behavioral equivalence.\\n     6\\t\\n     7\\tAuthorized internal rename target for preserved-behavior: formatInvoice -> formatAmount. Calculator cache is observable through inspectCachedTotal; changing it requires checking that consumer.\\ne7fbeceb20469acf0c73a0e946f85e3ce51d0b37 Seed isolated workflow inputs\\n^e7fbece (Workflow fixture 2026-10-16 21:16:03 +0300  1) let lastFormatted = '';\\n^e7fbece (Workflow fixture 2026-10-16 21:16:03 +0300  2) function fixed(value) { return value.toFixed(2); }\\n^e7fbece (Workflow fixture 2026-10-16 21:16:03 +0300  3) export function formatInvoice(value) {\\n^e7fbece (Workflow fixture 2026-10-16 21:16:03 +0300  4)   if (!Number.isFinite(value)) throw new Error('nonfinite');\\n^e7fbece (Workflow fixture 2026-10-16 21:16:03 +0300  5)   lastFormatted = fixed(value);\\n^e7fbece (Workflow fixture 2026-10-16 21:16:03 +0300  6)   return lastFormatted;\\n^e7fbece (Workflow fixture 2026-10-16 21:16:03 +0300  7) }\\n^e7fbece (Workflow fixture 2026-10-16 21:16:03 +0300  8) export function formatRows(rows) { return rows.map(row => formatInvoice(row.total)); }\\n^e7fbece (Workflow fixture 2026-10-16 21:16:03 +0300  9) // Separate known bug, outside formatter refactor: this sorts numeric strings lexically.\\n^e7fbece (Workflow fixture 2026-10-16 21:16:03 +0300 10) export function sortAmounts(values) { return values.toSorted(); }\\n\"}}\n"

      "text": "{\"args\":{\"cmd\":\"bun -e 'const c=await Bun.file(\\\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/read-only-answer/task-contract.json\\\").json();for(const p of c.sourceFiles.slice(18)){console.log(\\\"\\\\nSOURCE \\\"+p+\\\"\\\\n\\\"+await Bun.file(p).text())}'\",\"max_output_tokens\":11000},\"result\":{\"chunk_id\":\"0ea02f\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000005791,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":6373,\"output\":\"\\nSOURCE /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/unslop/SKILL.md\\n---\\nname: unslop\\ndescription: Cut AI tells from any writing. Must always apply.\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Unslop\\n\\nEdit text to remove AI patterns.\\n\\n## Process\\n\\n1. Scan for the patterns below.\\n2. Rewrite. Preserve meaning, match intended tone.\\n\\n## Patterns to detect and fix\\n\\nRule numbers are stable ids that other skills cite. A removed rule leaves a gap.\\n\\n### Content\\n\\n3. **Superficial -ing phrases.** \\\"highlighting...\\\", \\\"ensuring...\\\", \\\"reflecting...\\\", \\\"showcasing...\\\", \\\"fostering...\\\". Delete or expand with real sources.\\n5. **Vague attributions.** \\\"Experts believe\\\", \\\"Industry reports suggest\\\", \\\"Some critics argue\\\". Name the source or delete.\\n\\n### Language\\n\\n7. **AI vocabulary.** Additionally, crucial, delve, enduring, enhance, fostering, garner, interplay, intricate, landscape (abstract), pivotal, showcase, tapestry (abstract), testament, underscore, vibrant. Replace with plain words.\\n8. **Fancy ways to say \\\"is\\\".** \\\"serves as\\\", \\\"stands as\\\", \\\"boasts\\\", \\\"features\\\". Just say \\\"is\\\" or \\\"has\\\".\\n9. **\\\"Not just X, but Y.\\\"** State the point directly instead.\\n10. **Rule of three.** Forcing ideas into groups of three. Use the natural number.\\n11. **Synonym cycling.** Protagonist, main character, central figure, hero all in one paragraph. Pick one, repeat it.\\n12. **False ranges.** \\\"from X to Y\\\" where X and Y aren't on a meaningful scale. List topics directly.\\n\\n### Style\\n\\n13. **Em dash overuse.** Avoid em dashes entirely. Use periods or commas only (no parentheses, no en dashes, no hyphen-as-dash substitutes). If a thought needs separation, end the sentence or use a comma.\\n14. **Colon overuse.** Colons are fine before a list or example. Not as mid-sentence connectors. \\\"If you're coming from traditional automation: instead of registering event handlers, you describe conditions\\\" adds nothing with the colon. Rewrite to let the point stand on its own without comparison framing. \\\"Describing when the scheduler should fire works best as plain English.\\\" Same meaning, no crutch punctuation.\\n15. **Boldface overuse.** Don't bold every proper noun or acronym.\\n16. **Inline-header lists.** The tell is a bold label and colon that restates the line: \\\"**Performance:** Performance improved...\\\". Convert those to prose. A bold lead-in that ends in a period, names the item, and is followed by genuinely new detail (\\\"**Schema in TypeScript.** Tables live in one file.\\\") is fine, not a tell.\\n17. **Title case headings.** Use sentence case.\\n18. **Decorative emojis.** Remove from headings and bullets.\\n19. **Curly quotes.** Replace with straight quotes.\\n\\n### Communication artifacts\\n\\n20. **Chatbot phrases.** \\\"I hope this helps!\\\", \\\"Let me know if...\\\", \\\"Of course!\\\", \\\"Certainly!\\\", \\\"Found the smoking gun!\\\" Remove.\\n22. **Sycophantic tone.** \\\"Great question! You're absolutely right!\\\" Respond directly.\\n\\n### Filler\\n\\n23. **Filler phrases.** \\\"In order to\\\" becomes \\\"To\\\". \\\"Due to the fact that\\\" becomes \\\"Because\\\". \\\"It is important to note that\\\" gets deleted.\\n24. **Excessive hedging.** \\\"could potentially possibly be argued that it might\\\" becomes \\\"may\\\".\\n25. **Generic conclusions.** \\\"The future looks bright.\\\" State specific plans or facts.\\n\\n### Jargon\\n\\n26. **Abstract metaphor nouns.** Substrate, wedge, vector, locus, vantage, nexus, primitive (as noun), harness (as metaphor), surface (as in \\\"API surface\\\"), bedrock, scaffolding (as metaphor), modality, paradigm, gold-plating, ratchet (as metaphor), evacuate (for moving code), endgame, north star, flywheel. These read as technical but usually have a plainer concrete word. \\\"Substrate\\\" becomes \\\"base\\\". \\\"Wedge in\\\" becomes \\\"add\\\". \\\"Vector\\\" becomes \\\"way\\\" or \\\"method\\\". \\\"Gold-plating\\\" becomes \\\"more than the job needs\\\". \\\"Ratchet\\\" becomes the mechanism's real name or \\\"a limit that only tightens\\\". \\\"Evacuate\\\" becomes \\\"move out\\\". \\\"Endgame\\\" becomes \\\"the last phase\\\". Pick the concrete word.\\n\\n### Plain speech\\n\\n27. **Say what it does, not how it feels.** \\\"the database stays close at hand\\\", \\\"SQL you can read\\\", \\\"types that follow your schema\\\" name a feeling. The fix names the mechanism or a number: \\\"`.toSQL()` returns the exact string sent to the database\\\", \\\"a column rename fails the build\\\". Ask what the sentence tells the reader to do or know, then write that. If you can't restate it as a concrete instruction, fact, or number, cut it. One more check: if the sentence could appear unchanged in another project's docs, it says nothing about this one. Cut it.\\n28. **Shorten or split dense sentences.** If the reader has to backtrack to parse a sentence, break it in two or drop clauses. One idea per sentence.\\n29. **Active voice.** Prefer it. Catch \\\"is/are/was/were + past participle\\\" and name the actor: \\\"queries are validated\\\" becomes \\\"the compiler validates queries\\\", \\\"the file is parsed by the loader\\\" becomes \\\"the loader parses the file\\\". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or genuinely doesn't matter.\\n30. **Cut adverbs, or use a stronger verb.** \\\"runs quickly\\\" becomes \\\"is fast\\\" or the number. \\\"significantly improves\\\" becomes the measured delta. An adverb propping up a weak verb means the verb is wrong.\\n31. **Prefer the plain word.** \\\"utilize\\\" becomes \\\"use\\\", \\\"leverage\\\" becomes \\\"use\\\", \\\"facilitate\\\" becomes \\\"help\\\", \\\"numerous\\\" becomes \\\"many\\\", \\\"in the event that\\\" becomes \\\"if\\\". The fancier synonym is rarely clearer.\\n32. **Mannered prose.** Metaphor or flourish where a literal phrase exists: aphorisms (\\\"wire it or delete it\\\"), rhetorical fragments for effect, personified code (\\\"the plan holds it\\\"), figurative verbs (\\\"rides along\\\", \\\"stands on\\\"), stock framing phrases. \\\"A dial worth turning\\\" becomes \\\"a parameter worth varying\\\". Say what you mean. Rule 26 covers the metaphor nouns.\\n33. **Over-compression.** Dropped articles, verbless fragments, symbol-speak, and abbreviations that make the reader decode instead of read. \\\"Parser rejects bad date → exit 2, no write\\\" becomes \\\"The parser rejects a bad date, exits with code 2, and writes nothing.\\\" Write whole sentences with their articles and verbs, and spell out arrows and abbreviations.\\n\\n\\nSOURCE /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/architect/SKILL.md\\n---\\nname: architect\\ndescription: \\\"Sketch types, signatures, and module structure before code, then stay in the loop while implementation fills in. Use for /architect, 'architect this', 'design this', or non-trivial work where jumping to code would lock in the wrong shape.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Architect\\n\\nDesign before implementing. Sketch types, function signatures, class shapes, and module boundaries with `not implemented` bodies and pseudocode. Synthesize across multiple model perspectives, then fill in code against the chosen sketch. If implementation proves the sketch wrong, throw it out and redesign.\\n\\n## Start\\n\\nOpen a todolist with one entry per phase before starting. If no task-list tool is exposed, keep the same phase list in the response and disclose that limit.\\n\\n1. Ground\\n2. Sketch\\n3. Agree\\n4. Implement\\n5. Scrap\\n\\n## Session capabilities and authorization\\n\\nRead [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Use only observed exposed tools within user authorization. Read and follow each required skill in full. If direct invocation is unavailable, disclose that limit and execute its complete workflow inline as reduced capability. A missing required skill remains blocked. Record worker identities, requested and observed selections, rejections, result artifacts and limits.\\n\\nIndependent exploration requires authorized workers with observed spawn and result inspection. Without workers, disclose reduced capability and produce at least two sequential, structurally distinct alternatives using the complete arena workflow and runner discipline. Sequential alternatives are not independent model perspectives. Unverified or identical observed models do not establish model diversity. Denied source reads block grounding; denied writes leave a read-only design and an explicit implementation block.\\n\\n## Phase A: Ground the problem\\n\\nBuild a real mental model of every system the new code touches. Run the **how** skill over the relevant subsystems.\\n\\nNaming a file isn't grounding. Produce the traced model `how` prescribes. If the design redefines ownership or layering, also run the **why** skill on the existing shape so the rationale becomes a constraint, not a guess.\\n\\nSkip Phase A only when the work is genuinely greenfield with no surrounding system to integrate.\\n\\n## Phase B: Sketch\\n\\nRun the **arena** skill with the design-sketch task and the Phase A grounding artifacts. Pass `references/runner-prompt.md` as each runner's prompt. Each candidate produces a design package shaped per `references/rationale-template.md`.\\n\\nTake runner preferences from an available user-supplied `architect runners` configuration, in place of arena runner preferences. When no architect runner preferences are supplied, request inherited selection per the model-role contract. Preserve the goal of distinct model perspectives when supported and verified; inheritance does not establish diversity. Resolve aliases and rejected entries through the complete **arena** Phase A rules and current model-role contract. Select explicit models only when the exposed operation supports them; otherwise disclose inherited selection and unverified diversity. If an exact selection is required but unavailable, block that selection instead of silently replacing it.\\n\\nDesign it twice. Require at least two structurally distinct candidates before synthesis, even when the first looks sufficient. This is the **exhaust-the-design-space** principle skill made concrete. Whole-shape alternatives, not point fixes inside one shape.\\n\\nScreen every candidate against [`references/design-red-flags.md`](references/design-red-flags.md) before synthesis. Assume the next contributor is an agent that sees only the files it opened, copies the nearest example, and takes the shortest path that compiles. Prefer the design where a change that looks right from one file is right for the whole repo.\\n\\nCompare viable candidates on interface depth. Prefer the design that hides more complexity behind a smaller, simpler public surface. A rich interface can keep call chains short by concentrating capability instead of scattering it across layers.\\n\\nArena returns one synthesized design package. The synthesis decision populates the rationale's \\\"Synthesis decision\\\" section.\\n\\n## Phase C: Agree (opt-in)\\n\\nDefault: proceed directly to implementation with the synthesized design when implementation is already authorized. No additional human checkpoint. Design-only scope does not authorize implementation.\\n\\nOpt in to a checkpoint when the invoker explicitly asks: \\\"/architect with checkpoint,\\\" \\\"stop and show me before implementing,\\\" or similar. Then surface the synthesized design and pause for sign-off.\\n\\nWith explicit commit authorization, the synthesis can ship as its own commit either way, as the \\\"scaffold first\\\" mode of the **foundational-thinking** principle skill. Planned and scoped breakage during fill-in is fine, per the **outcome-oriented-execution** principle skill. For adversarial pressure on the design before implementing, run the **interrogate** skill on the synthesized sketch.\\n\\nIf the human pushes back on the shape (in a checkpoint or after the fact), treat that as Phase A evidence. Re-ground and re-run Phase B before writing more code.\\n\\n## Phase D: Implement against the sketch\\n\\nReplace `not implemented` bodies with code, pseudocode with logic. The synthesized sketch is the contract.\\n\\nDeviations from the sketch are signal worth surfacing, not friction to absorb silently. If a function needs a parameter the sketch didn't anticipate, ask whether the sketch was wrong, the requirement was missed, or the implementation is overreaching.\\n\\n## Phase E: Scrap when the architecture is wrong\\n\\nIf implementation keeps producing friction the sketch can't absorb, throw the sketch out. Don't bolt fixes onto a wrong design, per the **redesign-from-first-principles** and **fix-root-causes** principle skills.\\n\\nThe signal is a *pattern*, not single instances. Tells:\\n\\n- The same shape of workaround appearing repeatedly across unrelated code.\\n- Multiple unrelated edge cases that all need special-case branches.\\n- Types that need escape hatches (`any`, casts, optional fields always set in practice) to compile.\\n- The \\\"we need a lock\\\" reflex when the sketch said the state wasn't shared.\\n- Callers having to know the abstraction's internal rules to use it.\\n- Two or more independent Phase D deviations of the same shape across the implementation.\\n\\nUse judgment. A few edge cases don't condemn an architecture. Some problems are legitimately complex. Complexity in the data is not complexity in the design.\\n\\nWhen you scrap:\\n\\n1. Re-run the **how** skill over what's been built.\\n2. Redesign as if the new constraints had been day-one assumptions, per redesign-from-first-principles.\\n3. Subtract before adding, per the **subtract-before-you-add** principle skill. The new sketch should be smaller than the old one before it grows.\\n4. Return to Phase B and re-run arena.\\n\\n## Outputs\\n\\nThe caller's usage is written first and the type sketch derived from it. One file with new types and signatures for small changes. Module map plus type definitions for larger work. The rationale ships alongside, shaped per `references/rationale-template.md`, including the usage sketch and the synthesis decision.\\n\\nApply [unslop](../unslop/SKILL.md) to generated rationale and reports without changing facts or principle meanings.\\n\\n\\nSOURCE /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/bug-fix.md\\n### Bug fix\\n\\n**You own this task. Plan, review, verify.** Delegate investigation and the fix to subagents, stay in the lead. Read the complete [mode](../SKILL.md), applied principle leaves and routed skills. Record these six steps before task-specific todos.\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Supply the complete [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) to ad-hoc investigators and code delegates; routed skills retain their own roles. Assign exact input revisions, scopes, ownership and success criteria. Inspect artifacts and review the diff yourself. Missing independent workers allow authorized sequential work only as reduced capability. Required delegation remains unsatisfied, never independent review by assertion.\\n\\nBe scientific. Every shipped line traces to runtime evidence. Belt-and-suspenders that \\\"might help\\\" is a hypothesis, not a fix. It does not ship. When evidence refutes a hypothesis, revert what it motivated. The smallest change the evidence justifies ships, nothing more.\\n\\n1. Reproduce it yourself on the matching surface via the control skill in the mode's Non-negotiables, even when a debug or instrumentation protocol says to ask the user to reproduce. Ask the user only with a stated, specific reason the control surface cannot reach the target, and only after driving it as far as it goes. If it won't reproduce directly, synthesize the trigger, tighten conditions, or instrument until it fires. Record the exact symptom, trigger, surface, revision, command or action, exit status where available, and output before changing production code. Missing control companions or denied execution leave the original reproduction unverified. A proxy check cannot satisfy the matching-surface gate.\\n2. Binary-search the cause. Form candidate hypotheses, then rule them out until one survives. Seed them with [how](../../how/SKILL.md) over the affected subsystem and [why](../../why/SKILL.md) for regression history, using their complete prompts and source contracts. Each pass takes the split that cuts the most remaining problem space, gets runtime evidence and eliminates candidates. When program state is unclear, add authorized instrumentation or logging and read it as the code runs. Don't guess. Drive a long or stubborn hunt with an exposed, authorized repeated-execution operation. If unavailable, perform bounded in-session iterations and disclose reduced capability, without claiming a background loop. Confirm the surviving mechanism with runtime evidence before the step-3 architect/interrogate fan-out.\\n3. Plan the fix. If it crosses a function boundary, [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) first, using its complete exploration, runner, rationale and screening contracts. Contested design follows the mode's [interrogate](../../interrogate/SKILL.md) gate. Delegate implementation to a subagent using the configured bug-fix role with a specific scope. The portable default is inherited model and effort. The canonical default `grok-4.7-xhigh-fast` is historical provenance, not current availability. Select a configured model only when supported by exposed tools and observed; retain rejected selections and disclosed fallbacks. Unknown actual models prove no diversity. No required independent operation means that gate remains blocked.\\n4. Verify on the same surface. The original repro now passes. Retain the same trigger and conditions and record the actual output after the bounded root-cause fix. \\\"Inconclusive\\\" or wrong-surface is not a pass. Flag it. Unit tests show branch behavior, not bug absence. Keep denied controls, missing companions and unrun checks explicit.\\n5. Stage the commits so the failing repro lands before the fix in git history. See [tdd](../../tdd/SKILL.md) for the failing-test-first cadence when the bug has a cheap local test path. Skip adding that test when it would be expensive, integration-heavy or unclear, and state the reason and closest executable regression check. This is the canonical [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md) principle skill, the failing test first and the fix on top. Commits, rebases and history changes require actual authorization and available operations. Without them, preserve the failing-before/passing-after artifacts and ordered delivery plan; do not claim that sequence landed.\\n6. Run [Opening a PR](opening-a-pr.md) in full when its dependency and required capabilities are available and authorized. Missing playbook or companion gates stop dependent delivery. Return checked local artifacts and the exact unperformed actions otherwise. PR creation and other external writes require explicit authorization. Opening a PR does not implicitly start Babysit.\\n\\n**Reply:** what was broken, root cause, fix, how you verified. Paste failing-then-passing repro output verbatim. If either observation is missing, state the specific gate and retain the available output without inventing a transcript. Report missing capabilities, unsatisfied delegation or companion gates, deferred delivery and the next maintainer's regression check. Apply the local [unslop](../../unslop/SKILL.md) contract.\\n\\n\\nSOURCE /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/feature.md\\n### Feature\\n\\nYou own the design. Plan, review, verify. Delegate implementation. Stay in the lead.\\n\\nRead [poteto-mode](../SKILL.md) in full before work. Follow its Comments rules and required companion gates. Discover authorized operations through [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md). Use [assignment and integration](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md) and [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md). Keep the playbook steps verbatim in an exposed todo list, or an authorized local checklist with the reduced binding disclosed. Invocation aliases name workflows, not registered host commands.\\n\\n1. Run [how](../../how/SKILL.md) over the affected subsystem. Ground the intended user outcome in traced source and caller behavior.\\n2. Run [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) for parallel design exploration. Follow its complete design and arena contracts. Preserve sequential alternatives only as reduced capability when independent workers are unavailable. Required independence remains blocked.\\n3. Write the throughput checkpoint as four todo items. Keep any dimension that does not apply, such as a single file or no fan-out, with `n/a: <reason>` rather than dropping it.\\n   - Blocking first steps. Gates run before fan-out.\\n   - Independent workstreams. Disjoint files, services, or layers parallelize. Shared writes serialize.\\n   - Shared mutable state. Default to splitting the target using [separate-before-serializing-shared-state](../../principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state/SKILL.md). Serialize only for real invariants.\\n   - Smallest safe decomposition. If one worker is best, name why.\\n4. Delegate code-writing to a fresh independent worker with the entire [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and exact file pointers. Use the configured feature role when supported and observed. Otherwise request inherited model and effort. Record requested and observed selections separately, with unknown for unobserved values. Assign exact file paths, a named data shape and its organizing structure, and success criteria tied to the user outcome. Choose the structure before the delegate writes logic under [model-the-domain](../../principle-model-the-domain/SKILL.md). Prefer a state machine over scattered booleans, a table or registry over branching, or a typed model over repeated shape assumptions. When implementation admits multiple valid shapes, such as error handling, abstraction layer or test structure, delegate through [arena](../../arena/SKILL.md). Its runners surface alternatives and its fresh cross-judge guards the pick. This gate is mandatory. There is no skip-with-reason escape, and Laziness Protocol does not override it. The gain is review separation, not lines saved. A worker forbidden to spawn owns the diff directly and returns it to the lead for the same review separation. It never waits on a nested worker or replies \\\"standing by\\\". Without authorized independent workers, direct implementation can be an explicit reduced result, but it does not satisfy independent review separation. The lead reviews the actual diff and owns the summary. Apply the mode's Comments rules. Make surgical edits and re-ground upstream-derived files against their source. Port shared-primitive improvements to all consumers and verify each. Commit each verified unit when authorized and after the mode's commit gates.\\n5. Verify on the matching surface using available authorized control tools and the mode's matching companion requirements. Tie observed evidence to the intended user outcome. \\\"Inconclusive\\\" or wrong-surface is not a pass. Flag it and retain the unverified gate. Compilation or structural checks do not prove the feature works.\\n6. Rebase into small, ordered commits and stack follow-ups when authorized and safe for concurrent work. Use [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md). Build, verify and commit each small unit before the next. An unrun required check blocks advancement. If commit or rebase is unavailable or unauthorized, return the checked diff and ordered delivery plan with those operations unperformed.\\n7. If the design is contested, run [interrogate](../../interrogate/SKILL.md) before shipping. Preserve its review rubric, independence and observed model-diversity limits. Sequential review does not become independent or multi-model review.\\n8. Run the Opening a PR playbook at `opening-a-pr.md` in this directory. This caller requires that complete playbook, its review gates and actual PR result inspection. If the file is absent, report the exact deferred dependency and stop PR-dependent work. Do not reconstruct it from this paragraph. External PR creation and push require actual authorization. Return a local reviewable result when those actions are unavailable or unauthorized. Never claim a local draft is a created PR. Opening a PR does not start Babysit.\\n\\nCode-coupled work, such as one feature or one migration, goes to a single owner with the checkpoint inline. That owner fans out internally after the blocking phase when authorized and supported. Parent-level fan-out is for slices that produce independent artifacts, such as audits, cross-subsystem investigations or competing experiments. Rewrite the checkpoint at phase boundaries. Spawn a fresh owner rather than chaining interrupts. Distinct owned paths and inspected results remain prerequisites for integration.\\n\\nReply with what you built, what you chose and why, the throughput checkpoint, and open decisions. Use tables for design alternatives. Name the end user's changed behavior and what the next maintainer inherits. Link observed verification artifacts and actual PRs. Disclose missing companions, unavailable workers, unobserved models, failed checks and deferred dependencies beside the affected claim. Keep incomplete gates visible.\\n\\n\"}}\n"
